AI帶動 GPU、ASIC需求持續成長，但進入 Agentic AI 則是帶動
CPU需求強勁成長，驅動半導體榮景延續。由於目前需求遠遠大
過台積電所能供應的產能，且持續朝先進製程應用，因此，台積電
目前 N2已在新竹、高雄量產，且 2027年產能將會擴增一倍，應
用於智慧型手機、 HPC、AI，且 A14 將會在 2027 年進行風險性
試產，2028年量產； N3方面也會擴增三個新廠，台南廠的進度符
合預期， 1H27可量產，亞利桑那州第二個 Fab(N3)在 3Q27也可
開始量產 (比預期提前 )，熊本廠於 2028年裝機量產。 由於台積電
美國大客戶及美國政府 希望台積電在美擴大產能，因此，台積電將
會在亞利桑那州以外的德州擴產，會由台積電自已營運 Fab廠，
不過目前尚未有正式結論。 雖然看似擴充相當大的產能，但台積電
都已由上而上的評估且有紀律的擴產，在電力等關鍵指標方面也
都已考慮進去了。

除了先進製程需求強勁外，先進封裝的需求也相對緊俏，因此，台
積電在後段將與 Global Foundry 合作，由 Global Foundry 進行
Interposer 的生產，以滿足客戶需求 ，台積電則是衝刺利潤率更好
的先進製程。

三. 上修 2026/2027年稅後 EPS至 111.36 /152.74元 
2026年受惠 AI及 Agentic AI 需求超乎  預期的帶動下，台積電將
2026年全年的營收由 YoY+40%上修至 44%(以美元計價 )，因此，
我們預估台積電 2026 年營收 55,936 億元，YoY+46.85%，毛利
率 66.55%，稅後淨利 28,877 億元，YoY+68.1%，稅後 EPS由
106.63 元上修至 111.36元。2027年在 GPU、ASIC、CPU、XPU
需求持續強勁的帶動下， 台積電營運仍會是強勁成長的一年，我們
預估台積電 2027 年營收 77,218 億元， YoY+37.17%，毛利率
67.53%，稅後 EPS由 142.76 元上修至 152.74 元。

四. 投資建議維持買進，目標價 3055元 
在 AI需求強勁的帶動下， 先進製程產能持續緊俏，台積電除了積
極擴擴 N3產能外， 2027 年 N2的產能將增加一倍 ，在高階產品
應用上將取得高市佔率， 且在毛利率表現方面，因 先進製程領導地
位的穩固吸引更多訂單， 訂價策略的改變， 及良率的改善，帶動毛
利率維持高檔，帶動獲利 持續大幅成長， 因此，投資建議維持買
進，目標價 3055元(20X2027年稅後 EPS)。

一.公司簡介  
台積電(2330)成立於 1987年，為專業的 Fabless 半導體廠商，為
客戶生產的晶片廣泛地被運用在各種終端市場，例如行動裝置、高
效能運算、車用電子與物聯網等。 2019年，台積電及其子公司所
擁有及管理 約當年產能超過 1,200 萬片十二吋晶圓。在台灣設有
三座十二吋超大晶圓廠、四座八吋晶圓廠和一座六吋晶圓廠，並擁
有一家 100%持有之海外子公司 —台積電（南京）有限公司之十二
吋晶圓廠及二家 100%持有之海外子公司 —WaferTech 美國子公
司、台積電 (中國)有限公司之八吋晶圓廠。 台積電於 2011年第四
季開始導入 28nm，且開始加大先進製程技術的投資，於 2H12以
平面電晶體結構為基礎，在 20nm導入加強版的 HKMG 等技術，
並於 2014年進一步量產 16nm FinFET( 鰭式場效電晶體 )，持續推
進摩爾定律，儘管摩爾定律在 3nm以下將面臨物理極限，台積電
透過先進封裝製程延長摩爾定律，為高端產品 /客戶提供最先進的
技術。為因應美國製造優先及半導在在地化製造的需求，台積電於
2021年開始在美國亞利桑那州建廠，預計 興建六座先進製程廠、
2座先進封裝廠、 1個 R&D中心， 4nm已於 4Q24開始生產，預
估 3nm 也將在 2026 年進行量產，並且因為 AI 需求強勁的帶動
下，將加速 2nm及 16A的進廠速度，預估有機會於 2027～2028
年開始陸續量產。

二.營運概況及展望  
1、發展矽光子技術解決資料傳輸速度不足及訊耗損失問題  
10/2022 ChatGPT 推出後，其廣泛的推理能力，與使用者在各領
域以文字內容的方式對答如流，帶給使用者前所未有的體驗與 驚
豔，使用者呈現爆炸 性的成長，推升生成式 AI快速發展，使得 AI 
Server 呈現供不應求的現象 ，其不論在外觀的設計架構抑或 內部
的零組件規格甚至資料傳輸的頻寬及速度均較一般傳輸的 Server
大幅提升，且內部各元件的規格須同步提升才能使 AI Server 的強
大效能發揮最大的效用。

過往 Server 內部資料的傳輸採用銅線進行傳輸， 由於 AI Server
對資料傳輸的頻寬及傳輸速度的需求大幅提升， 銅線傳輸將產生
較大的訊號減損，使資料傳輸的品質不佳， 而以光做為資料傳輸的
解決方案，可解決資料傳輸訊號減損的問題， 因此，銅退光進的解
決方案勢在必行 。

矽光子的技術為銅退光進的最終解決方案， 但礙於技術及良率尚
不成熟，因此， CPO將為先行發展的技術，以 作為逐步達成光通
訊元件與主晶片直接在矽晶圓製程的階段即同時完成製作 的過渡
技術。在矽光子製作的過程中將以光波導取代 傳統銅線的傳輸，由

於光會沿著折射率較大的 物質前進，空氣的折射率為 1、水的折射
率為 1.33、玻璃的折射率為 1.46、二氧化矽的折射率為 1.6、矽
的折射率為 3.5，因此，光波導將選擇從矽晶圓上製作， 使光能進
行最佳的全反射，讓光能在光波導中的傳 送達最大的效率。由於矽
光子的發展為半導體未來的趨勢，大多數晶圓廠已投入研發，台積
電在此一趨勢下亦不缺席， 進行 40nm～90nm製程的矽光子技術
開發，預估 2025 年將利用 65nm 製程完成緊湊型通用光子引擎
(COUPE)的開發，支援可插拔式的連接器， 2026 年整合 CoWoS
封裝，將 CPO 與 Switch 晶片共同整合於同一基板上，完成共同
封裝光學 (CPO)的開發，未來將進一步將 CPO 與 XPU 共同整合
於矽仲介板上 ，實現晶片間更快的傳輸速度及較低的延時性。

圖一、矽光子發展示 意圖

資料來源： imec；宏遠投顧整理

2、透過 2.5D/3D 封裝延續摩爾定律  
矽為半導體重要材料，矽 1.0為平面式電晶 (Planar FET) 體時代，
由於電子遷移速度受到限制，製程節點 /閘極長度走到大約 28nm；
在 28nm以下進入到矽 2.0，為了改善電子元件漏電的問題， Intel
自 22nm 開始將平面世電晶體改成鰭式場效電晶體 (FinFET)，台
積電亦自 16nm開始將製程改為 FinFET，但當製程節點不斷往下
微縮至 5nm、3nm時，FinFET 將面臨摩爾定律的物理極限，即進
入矽 3.0時代，利用 2.5D及 3D封裝的技術可延續摩爾定律，台
積電在 2.5D封裝方面推出 CoWoS，Intel 推出 EMIB。CoWoS 在
晶片及基板之間加入 Si Interposer( 不具導電功能 )做為連接橋樑；
而 Intel 的 EMIB 則利用矽橋 (Silicon Bridge ；具有元件連通距離
短及反應速度快的特性 )做為多晶片之間連接的橋樑。

圖二、2.5D封裝示意圖

資料來源： Intel & 宏遠投顧彙整

在 3D封裝方面，台積電推出 3D Fabric 平台，Intel 推出 Foveros 
3D封裝架構。台積電 3D Fabric，主要為前段製程採用 Wafer on 
Wafer 及 Chip on Wafer 的 SoIC(系統整合單晶片 )方式，將晶片
進行 3D堆疊，然後結合後段先進封裝製程，包括 CoWoS及InFO，
將邏輯晶片及記憶體晶片進行 2.5D及 3D的封裝製程，達到縮小
IC 面積及提高效能的效果。而 Intel 的 Foveros 將不同製程節點
的晶片進行 3D封裝，包括 10nm製程的運算晶片、 22nm製程的
系統 I/O 晶片和 PoP(Package -on-Package)封裝的 DRAM 記憶
體。

## Document 099: 261012_gs_airtac

### Page 2

For the month of October 2026, we estimate AirTAC’s monthly revenue will decrease 
to Rmb729mn (-10% MoM, +16% YoY)/NT$3,447mn (-10% MoM, +28% YoY) 
We expect October revenue will go down by 10% MoM to Rmb729mn/NT$3,447mn, 
mainly due to 19% fewer working days in Mainland China. However, we expect to see 
improvement in daily pneumatic revenue (Rmb32.3mn in Oct vs. Rmb 29.4mn in Sept) as 
the improvement in daily pneumatic revenue is driven by the strong growth from key 
customers, including electronics, followed by machine tools and battery, and we further 
expect demand to improve following the end of low season.

Earnings and TP revisions 
We revise down our 2026E earnings estimates by 1% to factor in actual 3Q26 revenue 
(revise down 2026 revenue by 1%). We revise down our 2027E/2028E earnings estimate 
by 1%/1% to factor in a more conservative assumption on battery and linear guide 
business (revise down AirTAC revenue by 1%/1% in 2027E/2028E). We continue to see 
the company focusing on cost control as well as experiencing a better product mix 
outlook, but we expect some offset by rising material cost (revise down 2026E/2028E 
OPM by 0.1/0.1ppt).

### Page 3

Maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925), 
based on an unchanged target P/E of 26x on 2027 EPS.

Investment view: Accelerating monthly YoY from smaller segments could be a surprise. 
Maintain positive view on AirTAC 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we continue to believe the company is a key 
beneficiary of the factory automation/industrial automation localization trend in 
mainland China, and we expect the company will also benefit from the prolonged 
upcycle supported by the ongoing government stimulus policies. We see multiple key 
end-segments’ performance tracking ahead of the company’s 2026 full year guidance of 
20%+ YoY growth (see here), with battery/textile/machine tools/electronics delivering 
55%/46%/41%/23% YTD YoY growth while total revenue in Rmb is tracking at 23% YTD 
YoY growth. 
We continue to see solid monthly YoY growth from smaller segments, such as energy 
lighting and textile. However, the battery segment, which is the key growth driver in 
2025 (with 89% YoY in 2025), is seeing its first negative monthly YoY growth since Oct 
2024, while other major contribution sectors, such as electronics and automotive are 
seeing decelerating monthly YoY growth. As a result, we remain cautious on the 
potential of exaggerated orders (including advance or duplicate orders) despite new 
orders intake continuing to outpace shipments, and we will continue to monitor the 
underlying end-demand closely.

### Page 4

Maintain Buy on AirTAC with a lower 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925). 
Investment Thesis - AirTAC 
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share 
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality 
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast 
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner 
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC 
continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing 
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and 
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure 
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring 
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to 
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring. 
Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its 
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the 
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to 
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E 
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC. 
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on 
our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle). 
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected 
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in 
mainland China.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Improving 3Q GPM helped by better mix — Dynapack's preliminary 3Q26 results 
showed margin recovery yet weak PC sales remained the key drag on the earnings. 
3Q26 EPS of NT$2.33 (+72% q/q and +49% y/y) missed our/BBG est by 45%/27%, 
although we believe the earnings shortfall was well anticipated given the 3Q sales 
miss owing to the delayed shipment of one of the BBU products and weaker PC 
demand. GPM improved q/q on a richer product mix, with higher-margin BBU 
making up c.50% of 3Q sales on our estimate, while OPM recovered to 10.7%, 
+4.2ppt q/q and +1.3ppt y/y. Nevertheless, the sales shortfall constrained op 
leverage, leaving margin improvement insufficient to offset weaker sales. 
PSU vendor shortages weigh on Oct; 12kW ramp to drive Nov sales rebound — 
Our industry checks suggest that Dynapack's major PSU vendor has encountered 
component supply shortages (related to power management IC) and therefore 
delayed BBU pull-ins, weighing on shipments of Dynapack's existing 3kW and 5kW 
BBU products in Oct. We therefore expect Oct-26 BBU sales to decline slightly m/m. 
Meanwhile, PC sales should recover sequentially and more than to offset the BBU 
softness, supporting a slight m/m increase in group sales. Looking beyond Oct-26, 
we expect accelerating shipments of the new 12kW BBU series from Nov-26 to drive 
a strong rebound in BBU sales.

Implications — We reiterate our bullish stance on the BBU sector despite near-term
volatility from external supply-chain constraints, which is likely to put pressure on
near-term sentiment. We view the component-driven delays in product pull-ins
primarily as a shipment-timing issue, with underlying demand remaining strong into
2027E. We also see ample opportunity for Dynapack to gain market share from

Panasonic, its major Japanese peer, particularly as power architectures migrate
toward HVDC. This share-gain potential remains intact, reinforcing our confidence
in Dynapack's medium term BBU growth outlook.

Dynapack International
Valuation
We assign a 32x target P/E to our 2027E EPS of NT$25 to derive a target price of NT$800. We value Dynapack based on 2027E
target P/E as we believe 2027E better reflects its earnings power after BBU capacity expansion. Our target P/E of 32x is based
on our forecast EPS CAGR of 66% in 2025-2027E and a PEG of 0.5x, which we benchmark against its major PSU partner, as we

expect Dynapack to grow alongside its partner.
Risks
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Dynapack. However, we believe such a rating is not warranted for
Dynapack due to: 1) the company is generating solid revenue and earnings growth over next 3 years; the underlying industry,
BBU for AIDC, also enjoys secular growth; 2) the business model that involves with product customization and strict
safety/reliability requirements would imply relatively benign competition and therefore stable pricing and margins; and 3) the

company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash position since 2019.
Key downside risks to our price target/rating are: 1) Slower-than-expected BBU adoption in AIDC, 2) Delay in PSU customer
qualifications, 3) Competition from larger battery or power solution vendors, 4) Safety, certification and reliability issues.
Panasonic Holdings
(6752.T; ¥3957.0; 1; 16 Oct 26; 15:30)
Valuation
To derive our target price, we use a global sum-of-the-parts (SoTP) approach. In the case of Panasonic Holdings, we reference

52 other companies in the Citi coverage universe to set a fair value PER for each business division, which we sum to derive our
overall target price.
Panasonic fair-value PERs for individual business segments range from 8x to 31x, and have a weighted average of 26.2x
(excluding subsidies in the battery business), taking minority interest and equity in earnings into account. We estimate
business-derived equity value of ¥5,000/share. We calculate the present value of the amount we expect Panasonic to receive

in battery subsidies over five years at ¥400/share. Combining these two values, we derive a target price of ¥5,400.
Risks
Risks to our target price include 1) unsuccessful management policies or strategies resulting in weaker-than-expected
business investment returns or sales growth, or conversely producing greater-than-expected results at a relatively early stage,
2) stagnation in the global economy or an upturn in it, and rises or declines in demand in the electrical industry, and 3) forex

trends. If these factors manifest themselves differently than we have anticipated, the share price may vary from our target
price.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 149: 261020_ml_nienmade

Source PDF: all report pdf/261020_ml_nienmade.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

南電 (8046 TT) 
ABF 報價優於預期，獲利再度上修

永豐觀點 
南電 ABF 報價漲幅優於預期，2027 年獲利翻倍上修，長線 ABF 供給吃緊至 2028
年態勢不變，獲利延續性可期。 
 投資評價與建議 
南電 3Q26 營收達成率 125%，暗示 ABF 報價漲幅優於預期，長線GPU、ASIC 以
及 CPU 在 AI 需求推升下趨勢向上，南電因報價以短約為主於漲價週期更能快速
反應獲利，研究部上調 2026-2028 年稅後 EPS 至 18.90 元、50.93 元以及 76.51
元，投資建議維持買進，目標價反映獲利上調至 1660 元(26X2027-2028 年平均
EPS)。

ESG 評析 
南電企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。

南電為專業 ABF 製造商 ：南電為國內專業 IC 載板製造商 ，1H26 產品別 PC(17%)、

為 AMD、Broadcom、Hisilicon、Samsung、Continental 以及 Delphi 等，主要
競爭對手為 Ibiden、Samsung、Shinko、欣興以及景碩。 
3Q26 營收達成率 125%，ABF 報價漲幅優於預期：南電 3Q26 營收 163.78 億元
(+20.6%QoQ，+49.3%YoY)，整體 3Q26 營收達成率 125%優於預期，另一方面
日前公布的 8 月自結稅後淨利亦優於預期 3 個百分點，暗示 ABF 報價漲幅優於預
期，研究部將2H26 報價漲幅由原先的 5-10%上調至 10-20%，研究部上調 3Q26
毛利率至 30.4%，稅後淨利 36.60 億元(+61.8%QoQ，+405.0%YoY)，單季稅後
EPS 上調至 5.66 元(原估 3.31 元)。 
ABF 供給吃緊延續，4Q26 營收 QoQ+13.3%：展望 4Q26，研究部認為 ABF 在
AI Server 高速運算、異質整合以及 Agentic AI 帶動 CPU 相關需求拉升下，供給
吃緊態勢延續，預期報價仍具 10-20%漲幅空間，上調單季營收預估值至 185.53
億元 (+13.3%QoQ ， +66.2%YoY)，毛利率 36.3% ， 稅 後 淨 利49.82 億元
(+36.1%QoQ，+314.6%YoY)，單季稅後 EPS 上調至 7.71 元(原估 3.88 元)。 
2H26 ABF 報價優於預期，2026 年獲利上修 48.7%：展望 2026 年，研究部原預
期 ABF 2H26 單季漲幅 5-10%，惟受惠需求優於預期，報價漲幅上調 5-10 個百
分點，搭配整體產業稼動率維持高檔，相關族群獲利具上修空間，考量南電產品主
要走短約更易反映供需，另一方面公司 ABF 營收占比亦達 60%以上，在報價優於
預期時對獲利反映更加敏感，研究部上調全年營收至 596.83 億元(+48.6%YoY)，
毛利率由 20.4%上調至 28.2%，稅後淨利 122.13 億元(+527.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 
18.90 元(原估 12.71 元)。

Agentic AI 助攻，ABF 需求更具延續性：過去 ABF 需求主要聚焦 AI Server 所帶
動的 GPU 及 ASIC 需求，隨著 Agentic AI 大量導入，LLM 於多工處理時 CPU 資
源耗用增加，同步帶動相關需求提升，根據 AMD 於 2Q26 法說預期相關 CPU 產
值將由 2026 年的 600 億美金上升至 2030 年的 2000 億美金，研究部認為在 AI
產品週期由 GPU、ASIC 擴散至 CPU 下，ABF 需求將更具延續性，搭配指標大廠
Ibiden(4026 JP)於日前法說訊息，ABF 至 2028 年仍供給吃緊。 
ABF 供給吃緊可望延續 ， 南電2027-2028 年獲利翻倍上修 ：Nittobo 獨供的 Low 
CTE 玻纖布在 AI Server 用 ABF core layer 板厚增加 50%，造成產能供給吃緊，
連帶排擠 BT 載板中 AP 及 memory 相關供應，研究部預期即便 3Q27 新產能開
出仍不足支應目前 AI Server 殷切需求，ABF 供給吃緊態勢不變。研究部上修南電
2027-2028 年 獲 利 預 估 ，2027 年 營 收 上 修 至39.8% 來到 914.11 億元
(+53.2%YoY)，毛利率 48.3%，稅後淨利 329.08 億元(+169.5%YoY)，稅後 EPS
上修 135.2%至 50.93 元。2028 年營收上修至 1212.26 億元(+32.6%YoY)，毛利
率 53.0%，稅後淨利 494.41 億元(+50.2%YoY)，稅後 EPS 76.51 元(原估 42.03
元)，整體獲利趨勢仍呈高速成長。

## Document 196: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

SK하이닉스 (000660)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상  
3Q26 영업이익: 상반기와 다르다. 억제 요인들의 추세 반전 전환 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 전분기 대비 32% 증가한 80.1조원으로 시장기대
치를 능가할 전망이다. 최근 원화강세 흐름 및 상반기 동사의 상대적 실적개선 속도 
열위에 3Q26 영업이익 추정치는 70조원까지 내려갔다 (컨센서스 74조원). 하지만 
이제 동사 출하량 및 판가의 탄력적 복원세가 경쟁사 보다 빠른 실적 개선을 이끌 전
망이다. 3Q26 DRAM과 NAND 출하량/판가 증감률은 각각 +13%/+22%, 
+4%/+15% 수준으로, 각 영업이익은 63조원/17조원으로 예상된다 (OPM 각각 
84%/69% 추정). 그간의 상대적 실적 열위는 이제 반전된다. 이는 1) 판가 전략에 
있어 중장기적 관점에서 고객 중심 유연성 극대화를 꾀했던 부분의 보상적 되돌림이 
발생하고, 2) 업계에서 제일 높은 HBM 판매 비중으로 인해 판가 상승률에 발목 잡혔
지만, 오히려 HBM4 본격 공급이 시작되며 이익기여가 본격화된다. 아울러 3) 모바
일 핵심고객과의 기성 계약이 산업 전반적 LTA 논의로 재검토되며 공급사에 크게 유
리한 조건으로 변화되어 가기 시작한다. 당사는 1Q27부터 모바일 메모리 판가 급등
을 전망하며 (그림1), 2026E/2027E 영업이익의 경우 264조원/454조원으로 상향조정한
다. 4Q26 내 시장 실적 추정치 상향조정 및 투자자 수급의 구조적 개선을 전망한다.

‘지능기술’ 시대, 수요의 가격 역탄력성 속 중장기 기회창출에 주목 
과거 B2C 사이클과 다르게, 금번 B2B 사이클은 수요의 가격 역탄력성을 보이고 있
다. AGI 도달을 위한 선착순 투자가 핵심이기 때문이다. 빅테크 중 낙오자가 발생하
기 전까지 경쟁적 대규모 투자는 지속된다. 최근 동사의 솔리다임 상장 추진보도에 
일부 우려가 제기되고 있지만, 투자자들은 그 이면의 중장기적 기회에 주목해야 한다. 
동사는 LTA 외에도, 다양한 파트너십을 추진하며 중장기 시장 예측력을 증가시키고 
있다. 솔리다임 상장 과정에서 유입되는 현금은 모회사 ‘SK AI co’의 지분교환 및 JV 
구축 등에 활용되리라 예상된다. 이는 중장기 수요를 ‘창출’시키는 개념이다.

순수한 주주가치 제고 목적 환원 재도출 예상. 적정주가 상향조정 
빠르게 증가한 현금흐름 중 ‘잉여’분은 주주가치 ‘회복’에 우선 배분돼야 함이 마땅하
며, 동사 역시 최근 공시를 통해 주주가치 제고와 주가의 저평가 인식을 드러냈다. 현
재 진행중인 40조원 자사주 매입 프로그램 종료 후 즉각적인 신규 환원 정책 도출이 
예상된다. 2027년 메모리 공급부족은 명확하다. 역사적으로 효율적 시장 내, 주가 조
정은 탄력적 복원으로 귀결되어왔다. 동사에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하며, 적정주
가의 경우 320만원으로 상향한다 (26E/27E 평균 P/B 4.0배 적용). 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 12

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 3,200,000원 
현재주가 (10.8) 1,681,000원 
상승여력 90.4%

## Document 206: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

작성자(한동희)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

## Document 297: Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Thematic)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

### Page 2

— Chúng tôi dự báo vĩ mô trong nước đối với: (1) tỷ giá USD/VND tới cuối năm 2026 đi 
ngang so với cuối 2025 nhờ nguồn cung USD tích cực (từ kiều hối, FDI giải ngân và nâng 
hạng thị trường vốn) và chênh lệch lãi suất VND-USD dù có xu hướng thu hẹp nhưng 
vẫn duy trì trạng thái dương; và (2) lãi suất huy động sẽ duy trì ở vùng hiện tại trong 
phần còn lại của năm và kỳ vọng có thể giảm nhẹ nếu lạm phát được kiểm soát và 
NHNN sẽ có biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh khoản thị trường tích cực hơn.

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH FROM LSEG

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 1, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 30.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.8%. 
 Of the 13 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 84.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 11.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 11.2%. 
 76.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 65 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 65 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 20.1. 
 During the week of Oct. 5, three S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.  2 
There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $773.2B 
from $760.7B). Seven of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the materials (-9.1%) and health care (-2.2%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage decreases 
in earnings, while the energy (16.3%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. Overall, share-
weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 1.6% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the health care (-1.7B) and materials (-1.5B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level decreases in 
earnings, while the information technology (8.3B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 12.5B since the start of the quarter.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 30.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.8%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $773.2B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.9B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer Staples 
sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (114.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $55.1B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.7B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (445.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (116.1%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 25.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (66.5%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $256.1B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $153.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (135.6%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (68.9%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 25.3%. 
The Consumer Staples sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $33.7B 
in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $32.6B in 25Q3. 3 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Packaged Foods & Meats (-6.5%) and Household Products (-4.2%) sub-industries have 
the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 6.4%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q4 is 28.5%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 25.5%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $804.2B in 26Q4, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $625.7B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q4. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q4. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Real Estate sector 
has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q4. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (102.8%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $49.2B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $24.3B in 25Q4. 3 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (230.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (129.3%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39.4%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (54.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $289.1B in 26Q4, compared to earnings of $186.6B in 25Q4. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (117%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (68.8%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 18.4%. 
The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (4.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $15.2B in 
26Q4, compared to earnings of $14.6B in 25Q4. 2 of the 13 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Telecom Tower Reits (-13.3%) and Multi-Family Residential Reits (-0.1%) sub-industries 
have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 7.1%.

The estimated revenue growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 11.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth rate declines  to 11.2%. The S&P 
500 expects to see revenue of $4826.7B in 26Q3, compared to revenue of $4324.7B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 
constituents) in 25Q3.

11 of the 11 sectors anticipate revenue growth for the quarter. The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate for the 
quarter, while the Health Care sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3.

The Information Technology sector has the highest revenue growth rate (39.5%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $764.6B in 26Q3, compared to 
revenue of $547.9B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher revenue than a year ago. The Semiconductors 
(87.7%) and Semiconductor Materials & Equipment (48.2%) sub-industries have the highest revenue growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are 
removed, the growth rate declines to 22.1%.

The Health Care sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (4%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $958.4B in 26Q3, compared to revenue of 
$921.2B in 25Q3. 1 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower revenue than a year ago. The Health Care Supplies (-3.1%) 
and Managed Health Care (1.2%) sub-industries have the lowest revenue growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed , the growth rate 
improves to 5.1%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 31 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, three positive

surprises and five negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

## Document 337: Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

23 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.
1 Recovery and Resilience Scoreboard
2 The EU’s Recovery and Resilience Facility: Experience and Lessons for the Next Multiannual Financial Framework, WP/26/114, June 2026
SOURCE: EUROPEAN COMMISSION, Recovery and Resilience Scorecard

What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.

## Document 354: Romania’s inflation problem_ why disinflation is proving so difficult

Source PDF: all report pdf/Romania’s inflation problem_ why disinflation is proving so difficult.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Romania’s economy has cooled, but inﬂation hasn’t. Years of
wage-driven demand, repeated energy and tax shocks, and
stubborn services inﬂation are making the road back to price
stability unusually slow
Valentin Tataru
A succession of shocks has kept Romania's inﬂation ﬁght alive
Romania has not looked like an overheating economy for more
than two years. Growth has stalled, wage growth has slowed
sharply, real incomes are under pressure, and ﬁscal policy has

tightened. Yet underlying inﬂation remains stubbornly high relative
to both the rest of Europe and the National Bank of Romania’s
(NBR) target.
This disconnect between a cooling economy and persistent
inﬂation is Romania's central macroeconomic story. While the
country is still undergoing some income and price level
convergence with the euro area – making a modest inﬂation
diﬀerential structurally normal – that benign catch-up has been
overwhelmed by much stronger forces. Years in which nominal

### Page 2

incomes and domestic demand outpaced productive capacity have
been followed by a succession of overlapping energy, tax, and
administered-price shocks, each feeding into wages, services, and
short-term expectations before the previous shock had time to
fade.
The headline ﬁgure is falling, but the
underlying picture remains fragile
Romania’s annual consumer price inﬂation fell from 10.4% in June
to 6.2% in August, before edging up to 6.6% in September (6.5% on

the EU-comparable HICP measure, with prices rising 0.77% in
September alone). Yet this drop overstates the underlying
improvement. Much of the decline reﬂects favourable base eﬀects
as the sharp 2025 increases in electricity prices, VAT, and excise
duties drop out of the annual comparison.
A better gauge is the NBR’s adjusted CORE2 index, which excludes
administered prices and volatile components. Down from 8.5% at
the end of 2025 to 6.2% in September, core inﬂation remains

elevated and shows little sign of decoupling from the headline rate.
This distinction matters. One-oﬀ VAT or utility hikes automatically
drop out of annual inﬂation after 12 months, even though the
higher price level stays in place. The real question is whether such
shocks leave lasting marks on wage demands, rents, service prices,
expectations and so on. In Romania, a meaningful share of each
shock has done exactly that.
A relay race of shocks
Disinﬂation has been so slow partly because Romania has rarely

enjoyed a clean window for one shock to fade before the next
arrived. The pandemic supply shock was followed by the 2022
energy crisis. Energy price caps softened the initial blow to
households but merely postponed the adjustment; their
subsequent unwinding was followed by VAT and excise hikes in
2025, resetting the price level again. Before those eﬀects could
wash through wages and contracts, a new energy shock emerged
in 2026. The NBR’s August Inﬂation Report cited geopolitical

### Page 3

tensions involving Iran and low Danube water levels – which
constrained hydro and nuclear output – among the factors pushing
fuel and electricity costs higher, prompting the bank to raise its end-
2026 inﬂation forecast to an optimistic 6.1%.
Because inﬂation is path-dependent, this sequence is more than a
statistical twist. A temporary shock that leaves wage negotiations
and pricing behaviour unchanged eventually vanishes from the
inﬂation rate; a shock embedded into contracts and expectations

does not.
Romania is unusually vulnerable to this dynamic. Food and energy
make up a larger share of household spending – and energy
represents a larger share of production costs – than in Western
Europe. The same global commodity shock therefore hits Romania
harder both mathematically and psychologically. Because food,
fuel, and utility bills are the prices consumers observe most
frequently, repeated spikes encourage households and workers to
anchor their decisions to the most recent inﬂation prints rather

than the central bank's target.
Services: where the shock becomes
domestic
In September, food inﬂation stood at 3.0% and non-food goods at
7.1%, while services inﬂation reached 11.1%. Services are the
natural home of domestically generated persistence because they
are labour-intensive and largely sheltered from international
competition. A manufacturer facing higher local costs can
automate, relocate, or lose market share to imports. A restaurant,

hairdresser, dental clinic, or maintenance ﬁrm cannot import its
service from Germany; its costs and customers are local, making
wages, rent, and domestic expectations the primary drivers of
pricing.
While the 11.1% services ﬁgure also reﬂects lumpy adjustments in
rents and regulated utilities, the broader message is clear: inﬂation
has migrated from energy and traded goods into prices set inside
Romania.

### Page 4

Wages: the legacy of a boom that has
already ended
Wages are central to this story, though their timing matters more
than their current growth rate. At the macro level, unit labour-cost
growth roughly equals wage growth minus productivity growth:
Unit labour-cost growth ≈ wage growth − productivity growth
When wage growth outpaces productivity, the cost of producing a
unit of output rises, forcing ﬁrms to accept lower margins, cut
hiring, or raise prices.

Romania experienced an intense version of this gap. Over the ﬁve
years to 2024, real wages cumulatively outpaced productivity
growth by more than 20 percentage points. Fuelled by sharp
minimum-wage hikes and a nearly 20% jump in public-sector pay,
nominal unit labour costs surged by an estimated 18% in 2024
alone.
That acceleration is now over. By August 2026, average net wage
growth had slowed to 5.6% year-on-year, below inﬂation, meaning
real wages are contracting, while hiring has softened. Rather than

an active wage-price spiral, Romania is experiencing lagged wage-
cost pass-through: service ﬁrms are still adjusting prices to the
massive cumulative cost increases of 2023 and 2024. While this
suggests unit labour costs will stabilise if productivity recovers,
structural constraints, such as emigration, demographic ageing,
low participation, and skills mismatches, remain unresolved
beneath the cyclical slowdown, ready to reignite wage pressures

once demand returns.
Imports: the safety valve that shows up as
a deﬁcit
When domestic supply cannot keep pace with demand, imports
act as a safety valve. Both household consumption and capital
investment in Romania are heavily import-intensive; when the
government raises wages, pensions, or infrastructure spending, a
large share of that demand leaks abroad.

### Page 5

For several years, excess domestic demand was absorbed not just
by higher local prices, but by a widening trade gap, resulting in a
current account deﬁcit of 8.2% of GDP in 2024 and 7.9% in 2025,
the largest in the EU. Imports thus prevented domestic inﬂation
from rising even higher, but at the cost of a severe external
imbalance and greater vulnerability to global commodity prices
and exchange-rate shifts. Inﬂation and the current account deﬁcit

are ultimately two sides of the same coin: domestic demand
persistently outrunning productive capacity.
Fiscal policy: yesterday’s accelerator,
today’s brake
Romania's ﬁscal deﬁcit and ﬁscal impulse are not the same thing.
The deﬁcit widened to roughly 9.3% of GDP in 2024, supported by
rapid increases in public wages, pensions and other expenditure.
That ﬁscal expansion contributed directly to consumption, labour-
market pressure and the external deﬁcit. The deﬁcit subsequently

narrowed to around 7.7% in 2025 and is expected to decline further
in 2026 to around 6.0%. The improvement reﬂects the
consolidation packages adopted in the second half of 2025,
including higher VAT and excise rates and freezes on public sector
wages and pensions.
The inﬂation eﬀect runs in two directions. Higher indirect taxes and
administered prices initially raise the CPI. But consolidation also
reduces disposable income, consumption and companies' pricing

power. The ﬁrst-round eﬀect is inﬂationary; the subsequent
demand eﬀect is disinﬂationary.
This explains how Romania can combine weak activity with still-
high inﬂation. Romania is currently caught between those two
stages. The inﬂation data continue to reﬂect the impact of higher
taxes, energy costs and regulated price increases introduced as
part of the adjustment, even as the same ﬁscal correction is
weighing on demand. Fiscal policy is now contributing to the cure,

but some of the instruments used to achieve the cure temporarily
worsen the inﬂation print.

### Page 6

The NBR and monetary policy: constrained,
not cosmetic
Despite inﬂation sitting well above the NBR’s 2.5% target,
expectations have not fully de-anchored. Instead, anchoring has
become horizon-dependent. Professional forecasters still see
longer-term inﬂation near the top of the target band (with the NBR
projecting 3.4% by the end of 2027), showing the target still
anchors the destination. However, households and ﬁrms base their
short-term pricing and wage decisions on the supermarket prices

and utility bills they see today, leaving the target with little grip
over the near-term route.
Meanwhile, NBR has held its policy rate at 6.50%, with the lending
facility at 7.50% and the deposit facility at 5.50%, since the two 25
basis point cuts delivered in 2024. Romania's monetary
transmission mechanism can be imperfect. A persistent liquidity
surplus in the banking system can push money-market rates
toward the lower end of the central bank's corridor, reducing the

informational value of the headline policy rate. But this does not
make the policy rate irrelevant. The NBR can manage liquidity
directly, while monetary policy also operates through bank lending
conditions, government yields, asset prices, expectations and –
importantly for Romania – the exchange rate.
It is also misleading simply to subtract today's inﬂation rate from
today's policy rate and conclude that monetary policy is loose.
Investment and borrowing decisions depend on expected inﬂation

over the period during which those decisions pay oﬀ. If inﬂation is
expected to decline toward 3.0-4.0%, nominal borrowing costs
above 5.0% imply materially positive ex-ante real rates. The NBR's
constraint is therefore not simply a reluctance to tighten. With the
economy near stagnation, ﬁscal policy tightening, and supply-side
shocks unresponsive to rate hikes, the NBR is sensibly relying on
ﬁscal drag and weak consumption to do the heavy lifting – even if

the lags are long.
The exchange rate: stabilising inﬂation,
delaying adjustment

### Page 7

The leu formally ﬂoats, but the NBR has historically limited large
EUR/RON movements. For an import-intensive economy, that
policy has clear beneﬁts. A large depreciation would quickly raise
the domestic cost of imported goods. Exchange rate stability
therefore acts as a second nominal anchor alongside the inﬂation
target. But it also changes the way the economy adjusts.
The euro traded near RON 5.09 in March 2026, around RON 5.23 by
May and roughly RON 5.34 by early October – a noticeable

weakening of the leu after years of remarkable stability not far
from the 5.00 level. Even so, the currency has moved far less than
domestic costs. When Romanian wages and prices rise persistently
faster than those of trading partners while the nominal exchange
rate changes only modestly, the result is real appreciation.
The European Commission estimates that Romania’s unit-labour-
cost-based real eﬀective exchange rate appreciated signiﬁcantly in

2023 and 2024. The IMF’s external balance assessments also
suggest that the currency may be materially overvalued, although
these estimates are subject to considerable uncertainty. Regardless
of the precise degree of misalignment, if labour costs in Romania
continue to rise faster than those of its trading partners without
corresponding productivity gains or nominal currency depreciation,
cost competitiveness will weaken and pressure on the external

balance will increase. Even so, Romania’s export performance has
so far remained more resilient than relative cost indicators alone
would suggest.
That leaves the NBR facing a genuine trade-oﬀ. Allow more nominal
depreciation and import additional inﬂation in the short term, or
limit depreciation and accept a gradual deterioration in cost
competitiveness when domestic costs rise faster than those of
trading partners. Romania has largely opted for a modiﬁed version

of the latter approach, managing to combine exchange rate
stability with rapid wage growth and persistent inﬂation for several
years. The needed adjustment did not disappear, however, but it
was largely redirected into real appreciation and a wider current-
account deﬁcit. A stable exchange rate also preserved the
purchasing power of rapidly rising incomes. That helped support

### Page 8

consumption growth and, given Romania's high import propensity,
channelled part of the demand expansion into imports and a larger
external deﬁcit.
Productivity: the only durable solution, but
one that takes time
Weak consumption and high interest rates can suppress inﬂation
temporarily, but the only lasting cure is expanding productive
capacity: better infrastructure, higher labour participation, stronger
skills, and a larger tradable sector.
Backed by deep integration into European supply chains and

substantial EU funds for transport, energy, and digital networks,
Romania has the tools to raise potential output. Yet infrastructure
investment carries a timing mismatch: building roads and power
grids creates demand for labour and imported materials today,
while the productivity dividend arrives years later. Even so, shifting
the growth model from consumption and nominal wage hikes
toward investment and tradable capacity is the only sustainable

path forward.
Conclusion: persistence, not de-
anchoring
Romania is no longer in the overheating environment that
created its macroeconomic imbalances. Fiscal policy is
restrictive, real incomes are shrinking, consumption is weak,
and growth is near zero. Yet the economy entered this
slowdown carrying a massive overhang of unresolved cost
pressures from 2023-24 and a fresh round of tax and energy
shocks. Consequently, the most important metrics over the

coming months will not be headline CPI prints, but adjusted
CORE2 and services inﬂation. If they begin to fall as weak
demand works through the economy, Romania is navigating
a longer but ultimately conventional disinﬂation. If they
remain sticky, domestic pricing has become backwards-
looking enough to require a much longer period of economic

slack – or sharper policy intervention.
For now, the evidence points to persistence rather than de-

anchoring. It is also important not to confuse Romania's
inﬂation diﬀerential with macroeconomic imbalance. As long
as the economy continues to converge towards euro-area
income and productivity levels, the Balassa-Samuelson
eﬀects are likely to generate a modest inﬂation premium,
particularly in domestically oriented services.
Romania is not trapped in a permanent high-inﬂation
regime, nor has the recent dip in headline CPI completed the
adjustment. Rather, the challenge is that a structurally

normal convergence-related inﬂation premium has been
compounded by years of excess demand, repeated cost
shocks and their delayed pass-through into domestic prices.
Until growth shifts more decisively from consumption toward
productivity and productive capacity, Romanian inﬂation will
likely retain its deﬁning trait: it falls, but slowly, and each new
shock travels further through the economy than
policymakers would like.

## Document 400: USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

forecast +3.4%, prior revised +0.7%

MAIN POINTS:
1. The core PCE index increased +0.25% in August, somewhat below expectations, and
the year-over-year rate increased 3.01%. We estimate that the Dallas Fed’s trimmed
mean PCE measure increased 0.16% month over month and 2.20% year over year.
2. Year-over-year core PCE was revised downward 0.36pp in July to 2.98%, primarily
reflecting the methodological revisions to the calculation of the portfolio management
(-0.23pp), computer accessories and software (-0.11pp), and legal services (+0.01pp)

components of PCE. These downward revisions were larger than we expected, mostly
reflecting a larger revision to the portfolio management component than we expected.
3. Today’s inflation report, especially the downward revisions resulting from the
methodological changes, imply that core PCE inflation is likely to substantially
undershoot the median FOMC participant’s forecast of 3.4% for 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis.
We now expect a 3.0% increase for core PCE in 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis. Coupled with

comments from New York Fed President John Williams yesterday that “with the policy
action we took at our September meeting, there is no need for urgency, and we have
time to gather more information,” we now think that an October hike is unlikely.  We are
pushing back the second hike in our forecast to December, and we see a strong chance
that the FOMC will ultimately conclude that additional rate hikes are unnecessary.
4. Real GDP was revised up by 0.7pp to +2.2% in Q2 (quarter-over-quarter annualized),

largely reflecting upward revisions to consumption (+0.4pp to +3.8%) and investment
(+1.9pp to +4.6%) growth. The strength in consumer spending in Q2 likely continued to
reflect the boost to consumer cashflow from larger-than-usual tax refunds, but we
continue to expect consumer spending growth to slow somewhat in 2026Q4 as the fiscal
support fades and the headwind from higher energy prices remains. Stronger
investment growth reflected an upward revision to private fixed investment (+0.5pp to

+9.0%), driven by data center investment in the nonresidential structures category
(+1.9pp to +0.1%), and an upward revision to the contribution from inventories (+0.2pp
to -0.5pp).
5. Reflecting today’s annual revisions to the national accounts, real GDP was revised up
by 0.4pp to +2.5% in Q1 (quarter-over-quarter annualized), largely reflecting upward
revisions to the contributions from consumption and exports. Revisions for prior years
were modest: real GDP growth on a Q4/Q4 basis was unrevised for 2025 (+2.0%) and

2023 (+3.4%), and slightly revised down for 2024 (-0.2pp to +2.4%) and up for 2022
30 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs

### Page 3

(+0.1pp to +1.3%). 
6. Personal income increased 0.2% in August, below expectations. Year-over-year 
disposable personal income growth reached 4.8% in August. Personal spending rose 
0.9% in August, in line with consensus expectations. The savings rate decreased 0.5pp to 
4.1% in August, after a large 1.6pp upward revision to the savings rate last month driven 
by an upward revision to the level of disposable personal income (+1.2pp contribution) 
and a downward revision to personal spending as a share of DPI (+0.4pp). 
7. According to the Advance Economic Indicators Report, the goods trade deficit 
widened by $13.7bn to $132.6bn (seasonally adjusted) in August, well above 
expectations. Goods exports increased by $3.7bn to $203.4bn, largely driven by an 
increase in industrial supplies exports. Goods imports increased by $17.4bn to 
$336.1bn, driven by a large increase in industrial supplies and capital goods imports. 
8. According to the ADP report, private sector employment rose by 90k in September 
(month-over-month, seasonally adjusted), slightly above consensus expectations. 
Employment in the services sector rose 59k, primarily reflecting a 55k increase in 
education and health services and a 22k increase in leisure and hospitality employment 
that were partly offset by a 16k decrease in financial activities employment. 
Employment in goods-producing industries increased by 31k, driven by a 17k increase in 
manufacturing and a 15k increase in construction employment. ADP’s new median 
annual base pay measure, which better tracks other measures of wage growth than its 
gross pay measure, increased 3.0% for job stayers, unchanged from the August pace, 
and 4.8% for job changers, 0.1pp above the August pace. We left our forecast for August 
nonfarm payroll growth unchanged at +80k (vs. +90k consensus) ahead of Friday’s 
release. 
9. The details of the personal income and spending report were firmer than our previous 
GDP tracking assumptions. While the trade-related details of the Advance Economic 
Indicators report were much weaker than our previous assumptions, suggesting notably 
stronger imports growth in Q3 than we previously assumed, the inventories details were 
significantly stronger. On net, we lowered our Q3 GDP tracking estimate by 0.1pp to 
+3.3% (quarter-over-quarter annualized).

崇越 5434 TT  ( 電子–電子通路 ) 
先進製程擴張為營運成長基礎 
投資與評價 
1. 光阻劑、矽晶圓、光罩等半導體核心材料需求持續強勁，另環保工程業
務受惠於大型專案認列，2Q26 營收優於我們先前預期。 
2. 綜觀 7~9 月營收皆維持高檔，研究部推估主要受惠於晶圓代工業產能
持續擴張，且另有漲價挹注營收表現，3Q26 營收為 236.7 億元。 
3. 半導體主力產品及新品需求皆見成長，帶動半導體材料業務持續維持季
增，研究部上調 FY26 光阻劑營收全年年增率達 30%以上。 
4. 研究部上調 FY26/FY27 營收獲利預估，預估 FY26/FY27 EPS 分別為 
30.72/32.34 元。研究部維持買進投資建議，目標價調升至 700 元，以
21x FY27 獲利預估。 
重點更新 
半導體材料業務仍為今明年成長主旋律 
半導體主力產品及新品需求皆見成長，帶動半導體材料業務持續維持季增。
研究部上調 FY26 光阻劑營收全年年增率達 30%以上。矽晶圓市場需求情況
逐漸轉好 。 藍寶石基板 、 散熱材料等新品亦開始於1H26 成長力道逐漸顯著 ，
使其他電子材料 1H26 年增率達 30%以上。研究部預期產品調漲效應將延續
至 4Q26， 帶動公司FY26 整體營運逐季走高 ，FY27 營運表現亦將優於 FY26。 
工程業務 2H26 進入工程傳統認列旺季 
大型案件有所斬獲，環保工程在手訂單約近 200 億水平。隨下半年進入工
程傳統認列旺季，研究部預期營收將逐季走高，預估 FY26 全年工程業務營
收將年增 20%以上。崇越持續進行海外布局，目前東南亞案件持續進行
中，亦逐漸朝美國 AI 領域工程拓展，今年將陸續於多國設立據點，奠定工
程業務及半導體材料業務海外發展基礎。 
先進製程擴張為營運成長基礎 
信越於先進製程穩固的市佔率奠定崇越營運成長基礎。受惠先進製程產能持
續釋出且 UTR 維持高檔，加上成熟製程於 2H26 復甦趨勢確立，晶圓代工
產業動能明確。在此背景下，崇越憑藉於光阻劑與矽晶圓的穩固市占，並積
極布局先進封裝材料，奠定產業龍頭地位；此外，工程業務營收認列持續改
善，疊加在手訂單持續維持高水平，挹注營運動能。考量半導體材料業務動
能提升，研究部上調 FY26/FY27 營收獲利預估。

訪談內容 
半導體核心材料需求強勁、環工業務受惠於大型專案認列挹注 2Q26 營收表現 
光阻劑、矽晶圓、光罩等半導體核心材料需求持續強勁，另環保工程業務受惠於大型專案認列，
營收呈現季增，使 2Q26 營收達 208.3 億元(YoY+22.6%/QoQ +12.3%)，創下歷史新高，亦優
於 我 們 先 前 預 期 的 194.1 億元。 產 品 組 合 轉 好帶動 毛利率 持 續 維 持 高 檔 ， 為13.5% 
(YoY+1.8ppts/ QoQ-0.1ppts)，符合我們先前預期的 13.3%。EPS 創下歷史新高，達 7.92 元
(YoY+65.5%/QoQ+15.8%)，優於研究部原先預期的 6.58 元。 
綜觀 7~9 月營收皆維持高檔，研究部推估主要受惠於晶圓代工業產能持續擴張，且另有漲價挹
注營收表現， 3Q26 營收為 236.7 億元 (YoY+34.9%/QoQ+13.6%)；預估毛利率將 持穩於
13.3%(QoQ -0.2ppts/ YoY +1.6ppts) ， 產 品 組 合 維 持 穩 定；預期 EPS 為 8.42 元
(YoY+46.7%/QoQ+6.4%)。 
表一. 2Q26 財報與原先預期對比                                     (單位﹔百萬元) 
崇越 2Q26A 實際值 原先預估 差異幅度 差異分析 
營收 20,825  19,410  +7.2% 
1. 先進製程與先進封裝持續維持高 UTR 
2. 環保工程業務受惠於大型專案認列 
毛利率 13.5% 13.3% +0.2ppts 產品組合轉好 
營業利益率 7.1%  7.1%  +0.0ppts  
EPS 7.92  6.58  +20.4%  
資料來源：崇越，國泰證期研究部整理

半導體材料業務仍為今明年成長主旋律 
半導體主力產品及新品需求皆見成長，帶動 半導體材料業務持續維持季增。 光阻劑業務營收於
1H26 年增率達 30%以上，研究部上調 FY26 光阻劑營收全年年增率達 30%以上。矽晶圓市場需
求情況逐漸轉好，受惠於先進製程及記憶體廠 UTR 平均達 95~100%，出貨價格持續依 LTA 進度
調整。除光阻劑、矽晶圓等隨先進製程產能開出，藍寶石基板、散熱材料及 Underfill 等新品亦開
始於 1H26 成長力道逐漸顯著，使其他電子材料 1H26 年增率達 30%以上。除需求成長外，在油
價持續 維持高檔下，日本信越開始針對部分產品進行漲價，依據產品類別不同，漲價幅度約
15~20%，其中例如 FOSB 受成本影響較大且需求高，售價可能提升將更為顯著，研究部預期半
導體材料產品調漲效應將延續至 4Q26，帶動公司 FY26 整體營運逐季走高，FY27 營運表現亦將
優於 FY26。

工程業務 2H26 進入工程傳統認列旺季 
大型案件有所斬獲，環保工程在手訂單約近 200 億水平，分布於台灣、中國、新加坡、越南、馬來
西亞各區域。2Q26 工程業務營收顯著回穩，顯示在手訂單已逐步轉化為實際營收。隨下半年進入
工程傳統認列旺季 ， 研究部預期營收將逐季走高 ， 預估FY26 全年工程業務營收將年增 20%以上 。
崇越持續進行海外布局，目前東南亞案件持續進行中，亦逐漸朝美國 AI 領域工程拓展，今年將陸
續於多國設立據點，奠定工程業務及半導體材料業務海外發展基礎。

### Page 3

先進製程擴張為營運成長基礎 
信越於先進製程穩固的市佔率奠定崇越營運成長基礎。受惠先進製程產能持續釋出且 UTR 維持
高檔，加上成熟製程於 2H26 復甦趨勢確立，晶圓代工產業動能明確。在此背景下，崇越憑藉於
光阻劑與矽晶圓的穩固市占，並積極布局先進封裝材料，奠定產業龍頭地位；此外，工程業務營
收認列持續改善，疊加在手訂單持續維持高水平，挹注營運動能。考量半導體材料業務動能提升 ，
研究部上調 FY26/FY27 營 收 獲 利 預 估 ， 預 估FY26/27 營收分別為 865.0/964.7 億元
(YoY+28.0%/11.5%)，毛利率分別達 13.4%/13.3%，FY26/FY27 EPS 分別為 30.72/32.34 元
(YoY+42.6%/5.3%)。評價方面，崇越目前交易於 19.4/18.4 倍之今明年獲利預估，研究部維持
買進投資建議，目標價調升至 700 元，以 21x FY27 獲利預估。

公司簡介 
崇越科技成立於 1990 年，為半導體製程材料代理通路商，主要業務包含 1)半導體相關材料：在
中國及台灣獨家代理日廠信越化學(4063 JP)的矽晶圓/光阻劑/其他製程材料 ， 崇越與信越合作關
係緊密，先後合資成立信越光電、台灣信越半導體股份有限公司、崇越石英等公司。另外崇越亦
代理 FUJIMI(5384 JP)的研磨液。2) 光電相關材料：包含LED/LCD/其他電子材料等。3) 環保工
程：以廠務廢水處理為主，另有機電工程、水回收等廠務工程，主要來自蘇州工程及台灣建越。
4) 綠色能源：太陽能系統工程整合服務等。 
從客戶面來看，半導體相關材料業務中，台積電(2330 TT)為其最大客戶，占比營收約 20%，另
有如中芯國際、聯電、美光等客戶。環保工程業務客戶則有記憶體、面板及其他電子下游產業。

### Page 4

ESG 政策 
崇越在 MSCI ESG 評鑑 BB，台灣永續評鑑中評級為 AA，公司治理評鑑前 20％。另外崇越為富
邦公司治理 ETF(00692)成分股，其中於「經濟面」表現尤其突出，特別是股東權益、董事會結構
與運作、風險及危機管理。 
 利害關係人溝通與重大議題管理 
崇越科技重視與利害關係人溝通 ， 日常各項業務均保持與利害關係人良好溝通與互動 ， 依循2021
年改版之 GRI 通用準則 ， 依永續性脈絡 、 重大性 、 完整性及利害關係人包容性 ， 進行鑑別 、 排序 、
確認與檢視等之重大議題管理流程。 
 風險管理 
崇越科技考量更多異常事件應變措施 ， 提早整合公司發展政策 ， 可於危機發生時 ， 成為存續企業 。
成立資訊安全委員會，掌管營運所需之資訊科技相關事項。此外，2023 年亦成立資安室，設置
資訊安全專責主管與資安人員 ， 負責資訊安全相關工作與落實 。 已規劃於2024 年導入 ISO27001
資訊安全管理系統，預計 2025 取證認證。

## Document 427: 【國泰證期研究部】漢磊(3707 TT)-中立轉買進(+21.6%)-AI挹注，終迎春燕-20261013

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】漢磊(3707 TT)-中立轉買進(+21.6%)-AI挹注，終迎春燕-20261013.pdf

EPS 以調整後股數 34.87 百萬股計算 
漢磊 3707 TT  ( 電子–半導體 ) 
AI 挹注，終迎春燕 
投資與評價 
1. AI 需求強勁，2H26 逐步復甦，SiC 競爭壓力減緩，去庫存亦已完成，
預估 2026 年營收年增 29.0%。 
2. 新增產能陸續開出，應用多點開花，產品價量齊升，預估 2027 年營收
年增 23.8%。 
3. 預估漢磊 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 0.99/4.77 元，考量整體產業趨勢顯著
下，以同業本益比區間中間偏上位置 21 倍為評價基礎(21X FY27E 
EPS)，目標價 100 元，由中立轉為買進投資建議。 
重點更新 
2026 年 Si 逐步回溫，化合物半導體需求跳升，預估營收年增 29.0% 
根據調研，漢磊在六吋需求與產能利用率均有改善，四、五吋廠過去產能利
用率長期偏低，包含原物料取得不易，8 月已停止生產，10 月完成設備與人
員轉移，將資源集中於高價值的六吋與八吋產品，在Si 產品方面，TVS 受惠
AI 伺服器保護電路需求增加及轉單效應， 目前產能處於滿載狀態(使用六吋)，
推估 2H26 較 1H26 成長約 32%，化合物半導體部分，GaN 亦受惠 AI 資料
中心需求，產能滿載，HoH 研究部推估成長 26%，目前客戶需求已達可供應
量的兩倍水準，SiC 部分先前因大陸廠商競爭激烈，亦面臨去庫存，產能利用
率上半年僅約三成水準，現因其他廠商減產以及去庫存完成，3Q26 已回升
至六成，研究部預估 4Q26 將進一步回升至 65%水準，低基期下 2H26 營收
HoH 將成長約 123.5%，整體預估 2026 年營收至年增 29.0%。 
AI 帶動 2027 年維持高速成長，預估營收年增 23.8% 
TVS 部分由於新增產能陸續開出，加上 AI 伺服器、高速介面及晶片微縮後增
加的靜電保護需求，預估 2027 年將成長 19.8%，GaN 應用由消費性快充擴
大至 AI 伺服器 PSU、48V／800V 直流電源轉換架構，考量生產效率以及供
應能力，在客戶需求維持強勁下，研究部預估 2027 年成長約 15%水準，至
於 SiC 應用由電動車主驅逆變器，延伸至車載充電器，AI 資料中心的高功率
PSU 與固態變壓器部分客戶已在開發相關產品，在基期低下，明年 SiC 營收
相較今年將翻倍成長，目前與世界先進合作的八吋的月產能約 1,500 片，漢
磊自有六吋 SiC 月產能約 5,000 片，合計約 8,000 片六吋等效月產能。整體
2027 年研究部預估營收將年增 23.75%，達 92.1 億元。 
買進 – 中立轉買進

### Page 2

訪談內容 
SiC 合作優於預期，需求逐步爬坡 
SiC 目前 AI 伺服器功率需求逾 3kW 至 8kW 以上，SiC MOSFET 用於 3kW 以上 PSU，可縮小
體積及降低散熱成本， 另外太陽能逆變器 、 儲能與其他高壓系統逐步由650V、750V 往 1200V、
1700V 及更高電壓發展，漢磊客戶已有 3300V 產品，SiC 在價格達到甜蜜點下亦往消費性電子
及小家電滲透。 
而與世界先進合作的八吋 SiC 設備已全數到位，首批試產片良率優於預期，目標年底前進入小量
量產，首波五個客戶準備 T ape-out，預估 4Q26 至 1Q27 陸續推進，漢磊負責客戶與技術平台 ，
將技術導入合作產線，世界先進提供生產線及製造服務，客戶關係及技術仍由漢磊負責。八吋目
前預估月產能約 1,500 片，漢磊自有六吋 SiC 月產能約 5,000 片，八吋面積約為六吋的 1.7~1.8
倍，良率也較高，估算八吋1,500 片約相當於六吋 3,000 片，初期合計約8,000 片六吋等效月產
能，透過較小規模投資，合作八吋產線有機會擴至月產 3,000 片，合計六吋等效月產能可超過 1
萬片。

表一、漢磊 2026 年修正預估與前次預估比較 
資料來源：國泰證期研究部預估

### Page 3

漢磊 ESG 政策 
漢磊主要供應元件及積體電路代工事業：雙載子積體電路(Bipolar IC Service)、高功率場效電晶
體(Power MOSFET)、混合型積體電路(邏輯元件)、高壓 CMOS、高壓 BCD、GaN 及 SiC 分離式
元件等。產業類別歸類在半導體行業中，依據專門為投資人設計的永續揭露框架 SASB 準則，半
導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水管理、廢棄物和危險材料管
理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、材料採購與效率。因漢
磊企業社會責任報告書尚未更新至 2021 年以及 2020 年，相關資訊揭露較少，故從已知資訊作
分析。

1. 節省能源消耗政策： 
漢磊所使用於辦公室及生產廠區的能源，包含外購電力及各類化石燃料相關統計如下： 
a. 天然氣：主要用於有機廢氣吸附及燃燒處理系統與餐廳。 
b. 柴油：主要用於緊急發電機，當停電、定期保養及緊急狀況時才啟動，非工廠生產所需直接能
源。 
c. 外購電力：提供公司內部照明、空調、動力等的系統運作。 
因應產品特性 ， 新增6 吋 SiC 製程產線及車用產品品質要求 ，2019 年單位用電量較前 2 年增加，
但總用電量仍呈現下降趨勢。能源的使用不僅會消耗地球之資源，同時亦會產生二氧化碳，造成
溫室效應。漢磊科歷年來持續進行各項節能技術，並進行相關設施之節能改善計畫，節能成效顯
著。

圖一、漢磊節能策略

資料來源:漢磊、國泰證期研究部整理

2. 水資源管理及水汙染防治： 
漢磊用水均來自於自來水廠供應 ， 對水源並無顯著影響 。 其主要提供製程 、 空調及民生消耗所需 。
2019 年因應產品特性，新增 6 吋 SiC 製程產線及車用產品品質要求，單位用水量較前 2 年增，
但總用水量仍呈現下降趨勢。受全球氣候變遷影響，漢磊科更積極推動節能減碳活動，除了積極
節水外，也持續推動節水專案及引進各方面節水技術，2019 年節水比率逐步成長至 10.19%。漢
磊科廠區之用水量除蒸發損耗及綠化用水外 ， 其餘生活廢水及製程循環廢水皆經由適當的污水處
理設施處理後 ， 排入污水下水道 ， 流入管理局廢水處理廠 。 廢水排放水質皆符合主管機關之要求 ，
對生態環境及當地社區無顯著衝擊。

### Page 4

圖二、漢磊節水策略

圖三、漢磊 2017~2019 年用水回收率

資料來源:漢磊、國泰證期研究部整理

3. 碳排放管理： 
漢磊廠內配合國家溫室氣體減量法推動及相關政策致力於降低溫室氣體的衝擊。依循 CNS 
14064-1 
及行政院環保署溫室氣體查驗指引、溫室氣體排放量盤查登錄作業指引，並撰寫溫室氣體盤查報
告 書 ， 對 外 揭 露 溫 室 氣 體 排 放 量 數 據。 半 導 體 廠 的 主 要 溫 室 氣 體 來 源 為 全 氟 化 物
（Perfluorocompound, PFC），以≤ 150mm (六吋)晶圓最佳之技術選擇是製程最佳化（Process 
Optimization） 及取代化學品 （Alternative Chemistries） 方式進行PFC 排放減量。另外漢磊之
溫室氣體排放主要為使用電力間接排放之溫室氣體約佔總排放量之 58.87%，對溫室氣體減量的
努力將著重於用電設備能源效率之提升。2019 年溫室氣體增加主要是因應產能提升需求，採購
新機器設備而至用電量增加，故溫室氣體排放量較基準年高。

圖四、溫室氣體統計圖

資料來源:漢磊、國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 5

4. 廢棄物管理： 
漢磊 2019 年產出廢棄物數量為 566 噸 ， 較2018 年減少 26 噸 ， 其中回收及再利用廢棄物為110
噸佔總量 19%； 一般事業廢棄物為199 噸佔總量 35%； 有害事業廢棄物為258 噸佔總量 46%。
將廠區廢棄物種類進行分析，2019 年回收及再利用部分較 2018 年減少 13%，一般事業廢棄物
則減少 4%，有害事業廢棄物則增加 16%，當中 42%為專管回收有害高濃度氨氮廢液，降低環境
廢水的污染，並更有效率的使廢棄物再進入生產鏈的循環中。因應環保理念，漢磊本著永續經營
的理念與遵守法規之要求進行廢棄物妥善處理(如.將原本以廢水處理系統處理的含氟廢液，轉為
回收及再利用方式處理)，以避免生產活動造成對環境之衝擊。

## Document 465: インド消費者物価（26年９月）～総合ＣＰＩは5.5％に上昇、食品高が一段と加速

Source PDF: all report pdf/インド消費者物価（26年９月）～総合ＣＰＩは5.5％に上昇、食品高が一段と加速.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

１．総合ＣＰＩは5.5％に上昇、農村部では6％台へ 
インド統計・計画実施省が10月12日に公表した2026年9月の消費者物価指数（以下、ＣＰＩ）に
よると、総合ＣＰＩ上昇率は前年同月比5.5％となり、前月の4.8％から上昇した（図表１）。総合
インフレ率はインド準備銀行（ＲＢＩ）の物価目標である4％を4カ月連続で上回り、許容レンジの
上限である6％に近づいた。 
地域別にみると、都市部では前年同月比4.9％（前月：4.3％）、農村部では同6.1％（同5.2％）
となり、 いずれも前月から上昇した。 とりわけ農村部ではインフレ率がＲＢＩの許容レンジの上限
である6％を上回り、都市部との差も前月の0.9％ポイントから1.2％ポイントに拡大した。 
９月のＣＰＩは、野菜・豆類を中心に食品価格の上昇が加速したほか、外食・宿泊サービスや交
通など幅広い分野で伸びが高まっており、 物価上昇圧力が一段と強まったことを示す内容となった。
一方、貴金属価格の影響が大きい身の回り品・その他の伸びは鈍化し、保健医療など一部のサービ
ス価格はなお低い伸びにとどまっている。 今後は、 食品や輸送コストの上昇が幅広い品目に波及し、
基調的なインフレ圧力をさらに強めるかが焦点となろう。

２．食品インフレは7％台後半に加速、野菜・豆類が急上昇 
食品・飲料の上昇率は前年同月比7.4％となり、前月の同5.7％から大きく加速した（図表１）。
このうち消費者食品価格指数（ＣＦＰＩ）は同7.7％となり、前月の同6.0％から上昇した。 
食品の主要項目別にみると、砂糖・菓子類が前年同月比13.8％、果物が同10.8％、調理食品が同
10.0％と二桁の高い伸びを示したほか、野菜・豆類が同9.2％、肉類が同8.7％、油脂が同8.3％、
（図表１）

～総合ＣＰＩは 5.5％に上昇、食品高が一段と加速

### Page 2

３．燃料・光熱費は伸び拡大、原油価格は再び上昇 
燃料・光熱費の上昇率は前年同月比 3.2％となり、
前月の同3.1％からやや上昇した（図表１）。エネル
ギー価格の上昇率はなお比較的低いものの、これ
まで総合ＣＰＩを抑制してきた効果は弱まりつつあ
る。 
インド原油バスケット価格は、２月末の中東情勢
の緊迫化を受けて３月に急騰した後、いったん低
下したものの、７月以降は米国とイランの対立再燃
などを背景に再び上昇し、直近では１バレル＝120
ドル台の高水準に達している（図表３）。為替相場も
26年央にかけてルピー安が進んだ後、足元では１ドル＝95ルピー前後の安値圏で推移しており、原油高
が続けば、輸入コストを通じて国内の燃料価格に上昇圧力が及ぶ可能性がある。 
一方、輸送関連では高めの伸びが続いている。交通全体の上昇率は前年同月比5.0％となり、前月の同
4.6％から上昇した。内訳をみると、自家用車などの維持・利用費は同7.5％、貨物輸送サービスは同
14.7％と高い伸びを示したほか、旅客輸送サービスも同3.8％と前月の同3.4％から上昇した。一方、輸送
機器の購入は同▲2.6％と下落が続いた。今後、原油高が長期化すれば、燃料価格だけでなく、物流費な
どを通じて食品やサービス価格にも上昇圧力が波及する可能性がある。

４．コアインフレは4.3％に上昇、物価上昇の裾野が広がる 
食品・飲料および燃料・光熱費を除くコアイ
ンフレは、前年同月比4.3％となり、前月の同
4.2％から小幅に上昇した（図表１）。 
分野別にみると、身の回り品・その他の財・
サービスや外食・宿泊サービスが引き続き高い
伸びを示した（図表４）。このほか、交通は前
年同月比5.0％（前月：4.6％）、衣料・履物は
同3.9％ （同3.6％） 、 教育サービスは同3.9％ （同
3.7％） 、 情報通信は同2.4％ （同2.0％） 、 娯楽・
スポーツ・文化は同2.2％（同1.7％）と、いずれも前月から伸びが高まった。一方、保健医療は同
1.4％と低い伸びにとどまった。 
（図表３）

### Page 3

５．ＲＢＩは10月に利上げ、物価上振れリスクへの警戒を強める 
９月は総合ＣＰＩ、食品・飲料、コアＣＰＩ
の上昇率が揃って高まり、物価上昇圧力は前月
より一段と強まった。先行きは、降雨不足が食
品価格に及ぼす影響に加え、原油高によるコス
ト上昇が幅広い品目に波及するか が焦点となる。 
食品価格の先行きを左右する南西モンスーン
の降雨量は、６～９月の累計で平年の約87％に
とどまった。一方、カリフ作物の作付面積は10
月２日時点で約 1.12 億ヘクタールとなり、 平年
を約１％上回ったものの、前年同期を約１％下回った。作付けはおおむね平年水準を確保したが、
降雨不足や地域的なばらつきが収穫量に及ぼす影響に加え、 今後の冬作物の作付けへの影響にも注
意が必要である。 
こうしたなか、 ＲＢＩは10 月７日の金融政策決定会合で政策金利を 0.25％引き上げて 5.50％と
し、金融政策スタンスを「中立」から「調整された引き締め」に変更した。ＲＢＩは降雨不足や原
油高、 物価上昇圧力の広がりを警戒し、2026/27 年度のＣＰＩ上昇率予測を 5.0％から 5.2％に引き
上げた。 ９月のインフレ率上昇により、利上げ後の政策金利との差でみた実質金利はほぼゼロ％と
なった（図表５） 。 
もっとも、足元の物価上昇には食品や原油など供給面の要因が大きく、 基調的な需要インフレが
全面的に強まっているとは言い切れない。 ＲＢＩは10～12 月期のＣＰＩ上昇率を 6.0％と予測して
おり、当面は物価上昇圧力の広がりやコスト上昇の二次的な波及を慎重に見極めるとみられる。 食
品インフレの高止まりや原油高が続き、コアインフレへの波及が一段と明確になれば、 追加利上げ
観測が強まる可能性がある。

 東海経済は、持ち直している
 7月の生産（東海3県、季節調整値）は前月
比＋2.2％と2ヵ月ぶりに増加した。生産水

 輸出は総じてみると足踏み状態。先行きは

賃金・雇用
7月の愛知県の名目賃金は前年比－0.5％と8ヵ月ぶりに減少。実質では同－2.4％と4ヵ月ぶりに減少した。

26年4-6月期の失業率は1.9％と低下、就業者数は826万人と前期から増加した。

## Document 474: コロンビア、右派政権誕生で対米関係改善、ペソ相場に持ち直しの動き ～原油高と高金利が支え、先行きは政策実現力とインフレ対応が焦点～

Source PDF: all report pdf/コロンビア、右派政権誕生で対米関係改善、ペソ相場に持ち直しの動き ～原油高と高金利が支え、先行きは政策実現力とインフレ対応が焦点～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 20 日

1/3 
コロンビア、右派政権誕生で対米関係改善、ペソ相場に持ち直しの動き 
～原油高と高金利が支え、先行きは政策実現力とインフレ対応が焦点～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ コロンビアでは、６月の大統領選挙で、治安対策の強化や対米関係改善を掲げたデラエスプ
リエジャ氏が勝利し、中南米で続く右派政権拡大の流れを象徴する結果となった。８月の新
政権発足後は、トランプ米政権による安全保障支援や麻薬対策での連携強化を通じ、前政権
下で悪化した対米関係は急速に改善している。一方、同氏は政治経験を欠くうえ、左翼ゲリ
ラ掃討などの強硬な治安政策、貧困対策、国家機構縮小や減税、エネルギー産業強化など幅
広い政策を掲げるものの、議会で与党が少数派であるため、政策実現のハードルは高い。 
○ 金融市場では政権交代をいったん好感したものの、原油高によるインフレやＦＲＢの利上げ
に伴う米ドル高を受けて、通貨ペソは下落圧力にさらされた。国内でもエネルギー価格上昇
やエルニーニョ現象による農業生産の低迷を背景に、生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が強
まっており、中銀は９月末に追加利上げを決定するなどタカ派姿勢を強めている。 
○ 足元では、中銀による高金利維持姿勢を背景に高利回りを求める資金が流入し、ペソ相場は
持ち直している。先行きは引き続き米ドル相場の動向に注意が必要なものの、原油高の長期
化による産油国としての交易条件改善や高金利を背景とする資金流入が期待される ことか
ら、ペソ相場が比較的堅調に推移する可能性も考えられる。

南米コロンビアでは、 ６月に実施された大統領選挙の決選投票において、無所属ながら極右政党
（国民救済運動）の支援を受けた弁護士のデラエスプリエジャ氏が勝利した （注１） 。中南米では
2020 年代前半、左派政権の誕生が広がる「ピンクの潮流」の動きがみられ、同国でも2022 年に初
の左派政権 （ペトロ政権） が誕生した。 しかし、 ここ数年はアルゼンチン、 コスタリカ、 ボリビア、
エクアドル、 チリ、 そしてペルーで右派政権がドミノ的に誕生するなどピンクの潮流は後退してい
る。背景には、コロナ禍を経た景気低迷に加え、治安悪化も重なる形で有権者の関心が変化してい
ることがある。デラエスプリエジャ氏は当初、泡沫候補とみられたものの、犯罪取り締まりの強化

注１ ６月22 日付レポート「コロンビア大統領選､強硬右派の勝利で親米路線に回帰」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/623334.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
を掲げて最終盤にかけて支持を広げた。また、ペトロ前大統領の下では、トランプ米大統領との対
立を理由に対米関係が悪化したが、同氏はトランプ氏の支持を公言して対米関係の改善を目指す姿
勢をみせたことも支持を後押しした。 
８月に新政権が発足すると、トランプ米政権は同国に対する 10 億ドル規模の安全保障支援を行
うことを明らかにしたほか、 麻薬対策での連携強化を確認するなど、 対米関係は急速に改善に向か
っている。一方で、デラエスプリエジャ氏は政治経験がなく、その手腕は未知数である。大統領選
では、政治経験のなさを逆手に取り、アウトサイダーとして政治変革を実現すると主張したほか、
派手なパフォーマンスや過激な言動を通じて、自らを力強さの象徴である「虎」になぞらえる選挙
運動を展開した。さらに、公約として、爆撃による左翼ゲリラの掃討のほか、コカインの原料とな
るコカの栽培地への除草剤の散布など強硬路線への転換を図る考えを示した。そのうえで、 大規模
刑務所の建設のほか、教育・医療・住宅の改善を通じた貧困削減を目指すとした。一方で、前政権
の下で財政状況が急速に悪化していることを受けて、 国家機構の規模を最大４割縮小して減税を行
うほか、 輸出の３割を占める原油や石油製品、 天然ガス関連産業を強化する考えをみせた。 しかし、
共和国議会において、同政権を支える与党は上下院ともに少数派にとどまるため、政策実現のハー
ドルは極めて高い。 
このため、金融市場ではデラエスプリエジャ氏の勝利をいったん好感したものの、その後は様子
見姿勢に転じる動きがみられた。さら
に、 イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油高が
世界的なインフレを招くなか、ＦＲＢ
（米連邦準備制度理事会） の利上げ実施
などを受けた米ドル高も重なり、 同国の
通貨ペソ相場は調整の動きを強めた。 同
国は産油国ではあるものの、 原油高によ
るエネルギーインフレの影響は避けら
れないうえ、 足元ではエルニーニョ現象
による農業生産の低迷が食料インフレ
を招くなど、 生活必需品を中心とするイ
ンフレ圧力が顕在化している（図１） 。 
こうしたなか、中銀は９月末に開催した定例会合において追加利上げを決定した。会合では、７
人の理事のうち４人が25bp、１人は 50bp の利上げを主張していたことが明らかになった。その後
も、 理事の間から政策金利を長期間にわたって高水準に維持する必要があるとの主張が示されるな
ど、中銀がタカ派姿勢を強めていることが確認されている。 
足元ではＦＲＢによる追加利上げ観測が残るものの、 中銀が高金利政策を志向していることを受
けて、ペソ相場は持ち直している（図２） 。先行きも米ドル相場の動向を注視する必要はあるもの
の、イラン情勢を巡る不透明感が続き、原油高の長期化も予想されるなか、産油国である同国の交
